=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N cells in a row, numbered 1 to N.
For each 1 \leq i < N, cells i and i+1 are adjacent.
Initially, cell i is painted with color i.
You are given Q queries. Process them in order. Each query is of one of the following two types.

- 1 x c: Repaint the following to color c: all reachable cells reachable from cell x by repeatedly moving to an adjacent cell painted in the same color as the current cell.
- 2 c: Print the number of cells painted with color c.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
\mathrm{query}_1
\vdots
\mathrm{query}_Q

Each query is given in one of the following two formats:
1 x c

2 c

Output

Let q be the number of queries of the second type. Print q lines.
The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th such query.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 5 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- In queries of the first type, 1 \leq x \leq N.
- In queries of the first and second types, 1 \leq c \leq N.
- There is at least one query of the second type.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 6
1 5 4
1 4 2
2 2
1 3 2
1 2 3
2 3

Sample Output 1

3
4

The queries recolor the cells as shown in the figure.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We need solve problem. Need provide final Python code. Need think thoroughly but final only code maybe with explanation? User asks final code enclosed code block. Need solve.

We have N cells line, initial color i. Query type1: x c: Repaint all reachable cells reachable from x by repeatedly moving to adjacent cell painted in same color as current cell. This is the connected component (contiguous segment) of cells of same color containing x. Repaint entire component to color c. Type2: count cells with color c.

Need process up to N 5e5, Q 2e5. Need efficient.

We need maintain colored intervals / components. Initial each cell distinct color. Operation: find component containing x (maximal contiguous block of same color), set all cells in that interval to c. This may merge with neighboring intervals if their color equals c. Need update counts of colors.

This is classic interval union/find / ordered set. Need support:
- find component interval [l,r] containing x and its color.
- remove/update interval(s) to color c, merging adjacent intervals with color c.
- maintain color counts.

Since operation sets entire component to c, number of components can decrease by merging but also? Repainting a component to c can split? Wait if component is maximal same-color segment. If we repaint entire component to c, no split within it because all same. It may merge with left/right if their color c. It cannot create split. Number of components non-increasing? Actually if component color already c, no change. If not, component becomes c and merges with adjacent c components, reducing count by 0,1,2. So components only merge. Initial N components. O(N+Q) merges. Need find component by point. Could use DSU "next pointers" for intervals? Since components only merge, we can maintain intervals in ordered map and DSU? Need handle arbitrary color changes and merges. Since components merge, number decreases. We can use linked list of intervals? Need find interval containing x quickly. Use sorted set of interval starts maybe. Python no built-in balanced tree. Need implement with heap? Let's explore.

Because components only merge, maybe we can use DSU on cells for components? Operation repaint component to c and merge with adjacent components of color c. Need know current color of component. If components only merge, DSU with representative can store interval [l,r] and color. To find component containing x, need map from cell to component representative. Since components are intervals and merge adjacent, we can use "next unprocessed" DSU? Let's think.

We can maintain intervals in a disjoint-set over cells where each component has a representative and interval boundaries. To locate component containing x, if we have array comp_id for every cell? N 5e5, updating whole component to c would require updating all cells in component if merging? But components only merge, and we can use DSU parent to map cell to representative. For each cell, find root = DSU parent. Initially parent[i]=i. When merging intervals, union sets. Then find(x) gives root. But after union, all cells in merged component point to root. Need know interval [l,r] and color for root. If we union adjacent components with color c, okay. But what about repaint component to c not merging? Need update color of root. If merging with neighbor, union. But if component color changes to c and no merge, just color[root]=c. No need update individual cells. Since find(x) root. Good.

However, to know adjacent components to merge, we need boundaries. For component root, store L,R. When repaint component containing x to c:
- r = find(x), color old.
- if old == c: nothing.
- else set color[r] = c.
- Need check left neighbor cell L-1: find(L-1), if exists and color[neighbor] == c, merge. But after merging, new component interval extends left. Then check new left neighbor? Actually if we merge with left neighbor, the component's L becomes neighbor.L. Need then check further left? Could there be a chain of adjacent c components? Since components are maximal same color, adjacent components have different colors. If neighbor color c, and current component now c, they merge. The neighbor's left neighbor had color != c (because neighbor was maximal color c) before merge. After merge, left neighbor of merged component is same as neighbor's left neighbor, color != c, so no further merge left. But wait current component color c and neighbor color c; neighbor's left neighbor cannot also be c because then neighbor would not be maximal. So one left merge enough. Similarly right. But if after merging left, the component interval changes; right neighbor remains original R+1? If merge left, R unchanged. If merge right, L unchanged. If both, interval [L_left, R_right]. Need check right after left? Right neighbor of original component is R+1; if color c, merge. It is adjacent after repaint; left merge doesn't affect R. So okay. Need union with neighbor root.

But if we set color current to c and then union with neighbor c, count[c] already includes neighbor and current after add. Good.

Potential issue: If old != c, but adjacent component color c, we set current color c, count update. Then union. If union chooses root maybe neighbor root with color c. Need ensure count not double counted. We don't update counts on union. Good.

Need maintain interval boundaries for root. When union, new L=min, R=max. Need if union by size chooses root, update its L/R. But what about old root's L/R? Not needed if not root. But find on cells in old component returns new root. Need parent of old root to new root. Fine.

Need find neighbor by cell index. For component root r, L = left[r], R = right[r]. If L>1, neighbor = find(L-1). Since components are contiguous intervals and adjacent components partition line. find(L-1) returns root of left component. If color[neighbor] == c, merge. Similarly if R<N, neighbor = find(R+1).

Need initial colors: color[i] = i, parent[i]=i, L[i]=R[i]=i, size[i]=1. Counts count[c]=1 for c 1..N. But c up to N. Good.

But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But if union with left and root changed, find(R+1) okay. But
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline

    N, Q = map(int, input().split())

    parent = list(range(N + 1))
    size = [1] * (N + 1)
    left = list(range(N + 1))
    right = list(range(N + 1))
    color = list(range(N + 1))

    cnt = [0] + [1] * N

    def find(x):
        while parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
            x = parent[x]
        return x

    def unite(a, b):
        # a and b are roots
        if size[a] < size[b]:
            a, b = b, a
        parent[b] = a
        size[a] += size[b]
        if left[b] < left[a]:
            left[a] = left[b]
        if right[b] > right[a]:
            right[a] = right[b]
        return a

    out = []

    for _ in range(Q):
        q = list(map(int, input().split()))
        if q[0] == 1:
            _, x, c = q
            r = find(x)
            old = color[r]
            if old == c:
                continue

            L = left[r]
            R = right[r]
            length = R - L + 1

            cnt[old] -= length
            cnt[c] += length
            color[r] = c

            if L > 1:
                nb = find(L - 1)
                if color[nb] == c:
                    r = unite(r, nb)
                    L = left[r]
                    R = right[r]

            if R < N:
                nb = find(R + 1)
                if color[nb] == c:
                    r = unite(r, nb)

        else:
            _, c = q
            out.append(str(cnt[c]))

    print("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
